\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)+b(v(t))\mathbf{T}(t):\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x-\langle l(t),\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\rangle=0,

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
.
Clearly (
3.58
) holds if
\Gamma_{N}\neq\emptyset
. Otherwise, it is sufficient to prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{Q}b(v^{n})\mathbf{T}^{n}:\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{Q}b(v)\mathbf{T}:\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

However, due to a lack of convergence results for
(\mathbf{T}^{n})_{n}
that are global in space, it is not clear how to prove such a result in the mixed boundary case. Hence, from now on, we only consider the case
\Gamma_{D}=\partial\Omega
and so, to obtain the energy-dissipation equality it suffices to prove that (
3.57
) holds.

Using weak lower semi-continuity and the fact that
k>1
, we see that, for every
\psi\in C([0,T])
such that
\psi\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\psi(t)\Big\{\int_{\Omega}\Big[\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n}(t))v_{t}^{n}(t)}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))+\frac{1}{2\epsilon}(v^{n}(t)-1)v_{t}^{n}(t)

\displaystyle\quad+2\epsilon\nabla v^{n}(t)\cdot\nabla v_{t}^{n}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\Big]+\|v_{t}^{n}(t)\|_{k,2}^{2}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big[\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v_{t}^{n}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\psi\Big\{\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2\epsilon}(v(t)-1)v_{t}(t)+2\epsilon\nabla v(t)\cdot\nabla v_{t}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\|v_{t}(t)\|_{k,2}^{2}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t.

If we can show that

(3.59)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big[\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v_{t}^{n}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big]\geq\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

then (
3.57
) follows.
At best, we have a weak convergence result for
(v^{n}_{t})_{n}
. Hence we need a strong convergence result for
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
in order to compute the limit in (
3.59
). At present, we only have pointwise convergence. We show that this pointwise result for
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
can be improved to strong convergence in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
.

We first show that
\mathbf{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}
strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
.
If
a\in(0,\frac{2}{d})
, we later improve the result on
(\mathbf{T}^{n})_{n}
to strong convergence in
L^{1+\delta}(0,T;L^{1+\delta}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
for a
\delta=\delta(a)>0
.
Using that
(v^{n}_{t})_{n}
and
v_{t}
vanish on the boundary of
\Omega
, we then prove that

(3.60)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big[\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v_{t}^{n}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big]=\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

The energy-dissipation equality follows from (
3.60
) using the aforementioned arguments. As a result, we conclude the existence of a weak energy solution to the strain-limiting problem with Dirichlet boundary conditions.

Lemma 3.9
.

Suppose that the hypotheses of Theorem
3.2
hold and additionally assume that
\Gamma_{N}=\emptyset
.
Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\mathbf{T},v)
be the solution triple from Theorem
3.2
and
((\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\mathbf{T}^{n},v^{n}))_{n}
the sequence of solutions to the regularised problem constructed in Proposition
3.2
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d}).

Proof.

Using the pointwise convergence result, it is enough to show that
|\mathbf{T}^{n}|\rightarrow|\mathbf{T}|
strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
.
There exists a constant
C
, independent of
n
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{2}}{n}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{Q}\frac{|\mathbf{T}^{n}_{t}|^{2}}{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{c(\Omega_{0})}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\frac{|\nabla\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{2}}{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C,

for every compact subset
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
. Applying the Aubin–Lions lemma to
(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{T}^{n})_{n}
, it follows that
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbf{0}
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
.

On the other hand, using the elastodynamic equation and the convergence results for
(\boldsymbol{u}^{n})_{n}
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{Q}b(v^{n})\mathbf{T}^{n}:F_{n}(\mathbf{T}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{Q}b(v)\mathbf{T}:F(\mathbf{T})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

By the pointwise convergence result and the non-negativity of the integrands, it follows that

b(v^{n})\mathbf{T}^{n}:F_{n}(\mathbf{T}^{n})\rightarrow b(v)\mathbf{T}:F(\mathbf{T})\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)).

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{n}:F_{n}(\mathbf{T}^{n})\rightarrow\mathbf{T}:F(\mathbf{T})\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)).

Using the local convergence result for
(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{T}^{n})_{n}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
, we see that

(3.61)

\mathbf{T}^{n}:F(\mathbf{T}^{n})\rightarrow\mathbf{T}:F(\mathbf{T})\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)).

We write

(3.62)

|\mathbf{T}^{n}|=\Big(\frac{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{2}}{(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|>1\}}+|\mathbf{T}^{n}|\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|\leq 1\}}.

The indicator functions
\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|>1\}}
,
\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|\leq 1\}}
converge pointwise a.e. in
Q
to
\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}|>1\}}
and
\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}|\leq 1\}}
, respectively.
Applying the dominated convergence theorem and the pointwise convergence result, we get

\displaystyle|\mathbf{T}^{n}|\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|\leq 1\}}\rightarrow|\mathbf{T}|\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}|\leq 1\}}\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)).

For the other term on the right-hand side of (
3.62
), the second factor is uniformly bounded by
2^{\frac{1}{a}}
and converges pointwise a.e. on
Q
. The first factor converges strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
by (
3.61
). Thus we have

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{2}}{(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}^{n}|>1\}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Big(\frac{|\mathbf{T}|^{2}}{(1+|\mathbf{T}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{(1+|\mathbf{T}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}{|\mathbf{T}|}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}|>1\}}

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{T}|\cdot\mathbb{I}_{\{|\mathbf{T}|>1\}},

with respect to strong convergence in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
. Thus the convergence asserted in the statement of the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Suppose that the hypotheses of Lemma
3.9
hold. Furthermore, we also assume that
a\in(0,\frac{2}{d})
.
Let
(\boldsymbol{u},\mathbf{T},v)
be the solution triple from Theorem
3.2
and
((\boldsymbol{u}^{n},\mathbf{T}^{n},v^{n}))_{n}
the sequence of solutions to the regularised problem constructed in Proposition
3.2
. Let
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
be a compact subset. For every
\delta>0
such that
a+\delta<\frac{2}{d}
, there exists a constant
C=C(\Omega_{0})
, independent of
n
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{1+\delta}+|\mathbf{T}|^{1+\delta}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C.

Furthermore,
\mathbf{T}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}
strongly in
L^{1+{\delta}}(0,T;L^{1+{\delta}}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
for every
\delta
as above.

Proof.

First we recall that there exists a constant
C=C(\Omega_{0})
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\frac{|\nabla\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{2}}{(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq c_{a}\Big(c_{p}\int_{0}^{T}\|(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|)^{\frac{1-a}{2}}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega_{0})}\,\mathrm{d}t-1\Big)

\displaystyle\geq c_{a}c_{p}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{T}^{n}\|_{L^{\frac{p(1-a)}{2}}(\Omega_{0})}^{1-a}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big),

where
p\in(2,\infty)
if
d=2
and
p\in(2,\frac{2d}{d-2}]
if
d>2
, by the Sobolev embedding theorem applied to the function
(1+|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|)^{\frac{1-a}{2}}
. We consider the case
d>2
. The argument for
d=2
is similar. Letting
p=\frac{2d}{d-2}
, it follows that

(3.63)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{\frac{d(1-a)}{d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C,

where
C
is independent of
n
.
We fix
\delta=\delta(a,d)>0
such that

a+\delta\in(0,\frac{2}{d})
, which is possible due to the assumption on
a
. A standard manipulation shows that

\displaystyle\frac{d(1+\delta)-2}{d-2}<\frac{d(1-a)}{d-2}.

Applying Hölder’s inequality with parameters
\frac{d}{2}
and
\frac{d}{d-2}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{1+\delta}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{2}{d}}\Big(\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{1+\frac{\delta d}{d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{\frac{d(1+\delta)-2}{d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{\frac{d(1-a)}{d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{d-2}{d}}

Integrating over
(0,T)
and using (
3.63
), it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}|\mathbf{T}^{n}(t)|^{1+\delta}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C.

By reflexivity, we deduce that
\mathbf{T}^{n}\rightharpoonup\mathbf{T}
weakly in
L^{1+\delta}(0,T;L^{1+\delta}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
.
Combining the weak and pointwise convergence results yields the strong convergence result.
∎

With these strong convergence results in mind, we return to focusing on proving (
3.59
). We use the strong convergence of
(\mathbf{T}^{n})_{n}
to first show that
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
converges strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)^{d\times d})
. Then we make use of the memory kernel property to show that
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
converges strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
to
\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))
. We combine this with the weak-* convergence of
(v^{n}_{t})_{n}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))
to deal with the limit in (
3.59
) away from the boundary of
\Omega
. We use the auxiliary result, Corollary
3.6
, to control the limit near the boundary. This is another place where we make use of the fully Dirichlet boundary condition.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let the assumptions of Lemma
3.9
hold. Then we have that

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\rightarrow\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)).

Proof.

First, we show that
\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\rightarrow\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))
strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
. From the proof of Lemma
3.9
, we know that
(n^{-1}|\mathbf{T}^{n}|^{2})_{n}
converges strongly to
0
in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
.
Using the dominated convergence theorem and Lemma
3.9
, it follows that
\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{T}^{n})\rightarrow\varphi(\mathbf{T})
strongly in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
. Using this with the strong convergence of
(\mathbf{T}^{n}:F_{n}(\mathbf{T}^{n}))_{n}
and (
1.16
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\rightarrow\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\quad\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)).

Now we prove the convergence result for
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
. By the memory kernel property and Jensen’s inequality, it follows that

(3.64)

\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))\leq\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha\mathrm{e}^{\alpha(s-t)}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}s.

By the pointwise convergence result for
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
and the strong convergence result for
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
, we have

(3.65)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big\{\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha\mathrm{e}^{\alpha(s-t)}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}s-\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))\Big\}

\displaystyle=\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha\mathrm{e}^{\alpha(s-t)}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}s-\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}(t))),

a.e. in
\Omega
, for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
. However, using the non-negativity from (
3.64
) and (
3.65
) with Fatou’s lemma and the strong convergence of
(\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n})))_{n}
in
L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega))
, it follows that

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))-\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}(t)))+\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\alpha\mathrm{e}^{\alpha(s-t)}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))-\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))+\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\alpha\mathrm{e}^{\alpha(s-t)}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}^{n}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))+\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\alpha\mathrm{e}^{\alpha(s-t)}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

Cancelling out terms from the left- and right-hand sides, and rearranging the remaining two terms, it follows that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}(t)))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}(t)))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

using Fatou’s lemma again for the second inequality. The convergence of norms and the pointwise convergence yields the required result.
∎

The final piece of information required before we prove (
3.60
) concerns the sequence
(v^{n}_{t})_{n}
. We show that the functions
(v^{n}_{t})_{n}
vanish uniformly on the boundary.
This allows us to overcome the fact that the convergence result from Lemma
3.11
is only local in space.
We require the following auxiliary result, the proof of which can be found in the Appendix.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
w\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)
be such that
w=0
on
\partial\Omega
in the trace sense. There exists positive constants
C
and
\delta_{0}
, depending only on
\Omega
, such that

\displaystyle|w(x)|\leq C\|\nabla w\|_{\infty}d(x,\partial\Omega)\quad\text{ for a.e. }x\in\Omega\text{ such that }d(x,\partial\Omega)<\delta_{0}.

Corollary 3.6
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
3.2
hold and assume additionally that
\Gamma_{N}=\emptyset
. Let
\delta_{0}
be the constant from Lemma
3.12
. For every
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
, define
\Omega_{\delta}
to be the set of
x\in\Omega
such that
d(x,\partial\Omega)<\delta
. There exists a constant
C
, independent of
n
, such that, for every
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

(3.66)

\|b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}((0,T)\times\Omega_{\delta})}\leq C\delta.

Proof.

For
x\in\Omega_{\delta}
and a.e.
t\in(0,T)
, we have

\displaystyle|b^{\prime}(v^{n}(t,x))v^{n}_{t}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\|b^{\prime}(v^{n})\|_{\infty}|v^{n}_{t}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|v^{n}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla v^{n}_{t}\|_{\infty}d(x,\partial\Omega)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta.

Taking the essential supremum over the left-hand side, we conclude the required result.
∎

Now we have enough information to prove (
3.60
). As a consequence, we see that the energy-dissipation equality holds in the case of fully Dirichlet boundary conditions, concluding the proof of the existence result for the strain-limiting Dirichlet problem.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let the assumptions of Lemma
3.9
hold and let
\psi\in C([0,T])
be fixed but arbitrary. We have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Proof.

Let
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
where we define
\delta_{0}
and
\Omega_{\delta}
as in Lemma
3.12
and Corollary
3.6
, respectively. Then, we have

(3.67)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))-\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))-\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))-\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}}\psi\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\Big(\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}-\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\Big(\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))-\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}|\psi|\Big(\frac{|b^{\prime}(v^{n})||v^{n}_{t}|}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))+\frac{|b^{\prime}(v)||v_{t}|}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}}\psi\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\Big(\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}-\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}}\Big|\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))-\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\Big|\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+C\delta,

where
C
is a constant, independent of
n
, using the
L^{\infty}(Q)
bound on
(v^{n}_{t})_{n}
from Lemma
3.7
and (
3.66
) to bound the integral over
\Omega_{\delta}
on the right-hand side.

Using the local convergence results and noting that
\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}
is compactly contained in
\Omega
, the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.67
) vanishes in the limit as
n\rightarrow\infty
. For the first term on the right, we use that
(b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t})_{n}
converges weakly-* in
L^{\infty}(Q)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
with the fact that
\psi\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\in L^{1}(Q)
. It follows that this integral also vanishes in the limit. This yields

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big|\int_{Q}\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v^{n})v^{n}_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}^{n}))-\psi\frac{b^{\prime}(v)v_{t}}{\alpha}\varphi^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\alpha\boldsymbol{u}))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\Big|\leq C\delta,

for every
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
, where
C
is independent of
\delta
. Letting
\delta\rightarrow 0
, we conclude the stated result.
∎

Corollary 3.7
.

Let the hypotheses of Lemma
3.10
hold and let
(\boldsymbol{u},\mathbf{T},v)
be the solution triple constructed in Theorem
3.2
. Then
(\boldsymbol{u},\mathbf{T},v)
is a weak energy solution of the strain-limiting dynamic fracture problem in the following sense. For every
\boldsymbol{w}\in W^{1,2}_{D}(\Omega)^{d}\cap W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{w}+b(v(t))\mathbf{T}(t):\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{w})\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\boldsymbol{f}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{w}\,\mathrm{d}x,

for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
, with constitutive relation

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\alpha\boldsymbol{u})=\frac{\mathbf{T}}{(1+|\mathbf{T}|^{a})^{\frac{1}{a}}}\quad\text{ pointwise a.e. in }Q.

The minimisation problem (
3.2
) holds for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
and the energy-dissipation equality (
3.3
) is satisfied for every
t\in[0,T]
. The initial conditions hold in the sense that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow 0+}\big[\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{1,2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)-\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\|_{1,2}+\|v(t)-v_{0}\|_{k,2}\big]=0.

Appendix A
Proof of Lemma
3.12

Let
w\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)
be such that
w=0
on
\partial\Omega
in the trace sense.
The domain
\Omega
is Lipschitz, so for every
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
there exists an
r>0
and a Lipschitz function
\gamma:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r)

\displaystyle=\{x\in B(x_{0},r)\,:\,\mathbf{Q}x=y=(y^{\prime},y_{d}),\,y_{d}>\gamma(y^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle\partial\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r)

\displaystyle=\{x\in B(x_{0},r)\,:\,\mathbf{Q}x=y=(y^{\prime},y_{d}),\,y_{d}=\gamma(y^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle\Omega^{c}\cap B(x_{0},r)

\displaystyle=\{x\in B(x_{0},r)\,:\,\mathbf{Q}x=y=(y^{\prime},y_{d}),\,y_{d}<\gamma(y^{\prime})\},

where
\mathbf{Q}
is an orthogonal change of coordinates. Defining the function
\tilde{w}=w(\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\cdot)
, we have

(A.1)

\displaystyle|w(x)|=|\tilde{w}(y)|

\displaystyle=|\tilde{w}(y^{\prime},y_{d})-\tilde{w}(y^{\prime},\gamma(y^{\prime}))|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y_{d}}\tilde{w}(y^{\prime},sy_{d}+(1-s)\gamma(y^{\prime}))\cdot(y_{d}-\gamma(y^{\prime}))\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{y}\tilde{w}\|_{\infty}|y_{d}-\gamma(y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathbf{Q})\|\nabla w\|_{\infty}|y-(y^{\prime},\gamma(y^{\prime}))|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla w\|_{\infty}d(x,\partial\Omega),

where the constant
C
depends on
x_{0}
,
r
and
\mathbf{Q}
, but is independent of
w
.

Next, we cover the boundary by finitely many balls of this form, that is, there exists
K\in\mathbb{N}
, points
x_{1},\dots,x_{K}\in\partial\Omega
and positive real values
r_{1},\dots,r_{K}
such that
(B(x_{i},r_{i}))_{i=1}^{K}
is an open cover of
\partial\Omega
, with each ball
B(x_{i},r_{i})
of the above form.
In this case, there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that
\Omega_{\delta_{0}}\subset\cup_{i=1}^{K}B(x_{i},r_{i})
.
Using (
A.1
), there exists a constant
C
depending only on
\Omega
such that, for every
x\in\cup_{i=1}^{K}B(x_{i},r_{i})
,

(A.2)

|w(x)|\leq C\|\nabla w\|_{\infty}d(x,\partial\Omega).

Hence, (
A.2
) holds for every
x\in\Omega_{\delta_{0}}
and so we conclude the required result.
